# Fish fingers

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="fish-fingers-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="fish-fingers-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="fish-fingers-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Fish fingers in the HMI library has 50 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, methylmercury, nickel, chromium and uranium from 5 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/bfr2024-methylmercury-fish-seafood-germany|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">German Federal Institute for Risk Assessment (BfR) · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bfr2024-methylmercury-fish-seafood-germany">Methylmercury in fish and seafood – health risk assessment of new data from the BfR MEAL study</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">BfR Opinion 023/2024</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/bfr2024-methylmercury-fish-seafood-germany">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury and methylmercury and others, in 50 results from 5 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the fish fingers was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>

<a id="hmi-ing-006"></a>

class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite">[[sources/bfr2024-methylmercury-fish-seafood-germany|5]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">German Federal Institute for Risk Assessment (BfR) · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/bfr2024-methylmercury-fish-seafood-germany">Methylmercury in fish and seafood – health risk assessment of new data from the BfR MEAL class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span →</a></span></span></sup> In the US Total Diet Study, fish fingers, cooked had a median lead result below 4 µg/kg across 3 composites from 2018–2020.<sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Spungen et al. 2024 reported lead at 2.7 µg/kg (mean) in fish fingers, cooked from all samples.<sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Across 2 other studies of ordinary farms and markets, reported cadmium means and medians on an as-sold or fresh-weight basis ranged from 6 µg/kg (Naess et al. 2020) to 12 µg/kg (Spungen et al. 2024).<sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the fish fingers came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as fish fingers

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

18 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 0 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-fish-fingers" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-fish-fingers-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of fish fingers, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Fish fingers, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤20</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤16</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>34 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>34</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for fish fingers, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for fish fingers, as sold on this basis in the completed fish fingers ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Limited data · HMI fish fingers Pb reference · Fish fingers, as sold. median as reported: ≤40 µg/kg. HMI median ≤10; Q1 ≤10; Q3 ≤20 µg/kg. 1 family. Reporting bounds prevent a rating.</p><p>Reporting bounds or insufficient reference spread prevent a stable Low / Moderate / High classification.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Limited data. The position remains unresolved under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:fish-fingers-23181639d0a6:15a2649314c6"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:fish-fingers-23181639d0a6:15a2649314c6">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2004–2019</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>EURO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. <a id="sources-and-contamination-pathways"></a>
## How metals reach fish fingers

<a id="hmi-ing-010"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-root-uptake-surface-deposition-feed-transfer-or-other-biological-pathways"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-011"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-012"></a>
<a id="which-source-attribution-claims-are-measured-modelled-inferred-or-unresolved"></a>

No study in the library compared fish fingers from contaminated and ordinary settings, so the routes by which metals reach it are not measured here.

The HMI reference for lead in fish fingers, as sold pools 34 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of below 10 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 10 (lower quartile) and below 20 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium in fish fingers, as sold, the reference median was between 7.5 and 8.5 µg/kg, with a middle half from between 2.5 and 5.5 to between 9 and 10 µg/kg across 29 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic in fish fingers, as sold, the reference median was 946 µg/kg, with a middle half from 724 to 1,270 µg/kg across 13 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, fish fingers, as sold had the lowest cadmium subgroup median in PAHO (9 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (12 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic, the lowest regional median was in EURO (268 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (1,450 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<a id="mitigation-options"></a>
## Mitigation for fish fingers

<a id="hmi-ing-019"></a>
<a id="which-prevention-mitigation-or-remediation-measures-have-been-evaluated-for-this-ingredient"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-020"></a>
<a id="under-what-conditions-at-which-stage-and-against-which-comparator-do-they-work-or-fail"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-021"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-supports-field-or-commercial-implementation-and-ongoing-verification"></a>

Lot testing against the applicable limit is the check that can be verified.

<p class="hmi-mitigation-gap" data-mitigation-gap>No measured way to lower metals in this food is in the library yet.</p>



<a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>
<a id="what-effects-on-nutrition-yield-sensory-quality-stability-and-waste-streams-were-measured"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing fish fingers for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup arsenic ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 3 µg/kg (FDA Total Diet Study), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying fish fingers

<a id="hmi-ing-028"></a>
<a id="what-origin-traceability-processing-and-analytical-information-should-a-buyer-request"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-029"></a>
<a id="which-control-or-specification-choices-are-supported-and-which-are-policy-choices-requiring-separate-justification"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-030"></a>
<a id="what-supplier-process-or-test-result-changes-should-trigger-investigation-or-requalification"></a>

No maximum level for metals in fish fingers was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from fish fingers

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-032"></a>
<a id="how-do-consumption-patterns-and-intended-populations-affect-dietary-exposure-interpretation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

No source in the library converted fish fingers concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.

<a id="requirements-evidence-assessment-and-updates"></a>
## Evidence review and remaining gaps

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>
<a id="which-primary-regulatory-instruments-apply-to-this-exact-ingredient-form-species-market-and-date"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-035"></a>
<a id="what-searches-eligibility-decisions-source-families-and-appraisal-underpin-the-answers-and-certainty-judgments"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-036"></a>
<a id="what-remains-unresolved-when-was-each-answer-reviewed-and-what-changed-in-this-version"></a>

Every source record that names fish fingers was decided on its content: 5 occurrence extracted, 5 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Fish fingers FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-fish-fingers" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-fish-fingers" data-question="What heavy metals are found in fish fingers?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in fish fingers?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, total mercury, methylmercury, nickel, chromium and uranium in fish fingers, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">U.S. Food and Drug Administration · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">FY2018-FY2020 TDS Elements Analytical Results</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA Total Diet Study</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2022-tds-elements-fy2018-fy2020">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Naess S, Aakre I, Lundebye AK, Ornsrud R, Kjellevold M, Markhus MW, Dahl L · 2020</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Mercury, lead, arsenic, and cadmium in Norwegian seafood products and consumer exposure</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part B</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/naess2020-norwegian-seafood-metals">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Spungen JH, et al. · 2024</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Infants&#39; and young children&#39;s dietary exposures to lead and cadmium: FDA total diet study 2018-2020</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Food Additives &amp; Contaminants: Part A</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/spungen2024-fda-tds-infant-lead-cadmium">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in fish fingers, as sold from 169 results in 3 reporting regions, with a subgroup median of 2.8 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-fish-fingers" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-fish-fingers" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in fish fingers?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in fish fingers?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, fish fingers, as sold had the lowest cadmium subgroup median in PAHO (9 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (12 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For total arsenic, the lowest regional median was in EURO (268 µg/kg) and the highest in EURO (1,450 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-fish-fingers-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-fish-fingers-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in fish fingers is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in fish fingers is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 1 result from 1 source, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported inorganic arsenic in fish fingers, as sold from 2 results in 1 reporting region, with subgroup medians below 11.9 µg/kg, mostly non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
